● I cloned the repo into relay-analytics/ with --depth 1 and deleted its .git,
  so there's no history left. I haven't run git init yet. One thing first: that
  folder is inside your qualitara-process worktree right now, so the parent repo
  will see it as untracked files. Tell me where the new repo should live and
  what to call it (the starter README asks you not to keep
  relay-analytics-starter), and I'll move it and initialize it. To profile the
  data I loaded it into a scratch SQLite DB.

  What we're working with

  Relay is a fictional B2B SaaS that tracks inbound calls, leads and
  appointments for service businesses with 1–15 locations. Today the dashboard
  shows only raw totals per location. Two things customers ask for: "is this
  number normal for us?" and "which location needs attention?"

  The repo is just context and data. There's no app code: two docs, schema.sql
  (2 tables) and seed.sql (20 accounts, 12,626 events). The README says to load
  the schema through your own migrations, use the data as-is (no regenerating
  it), and treat it as messy production data.

  Schema

  mermaid
  erDiagram
      accounts ||--o{ activity_events : has
      accounts {
          INTEGER id PK
          VARCHAR name
          VARCHAR industry
          VARCHAR timezone "IANA"
          TIMESTAMP created_at "UTC"
      }
      activity_events {
          INTEGER id PK
          INTEGER account_id FK
          VARCHAR location "free text, 'Site A'..'Site O'"
          VARCHAR event_type "call_received | lead_created | appointment_set"
          TIMESTAMP occurred_at "UTC"
          INTEGER duration_seconds "nullable"
          VARCHAR outcome "nullable"
      }

  There's no locations table. A location is just a label, and it only means
  something together with account_id ("Site A" of account 1 is a different place
  from "Site A" of account 6). The schema also has no indexes and no CHECK
  constraints.

  The data

  - Time range: 2026-02-01 to 2026-07-27 UTC, about 26 weeks. July is a partial
    month.
  - Accounts: 19 of the 20 have events. Sizes run from 167 to 2,641 events and 1
    to 15 locations. Metro Collision (account 6, 15 sites) is the big one.
  - Event mix:

  | Event type      | Count | Outcomes                       |
  |-----------------|-------|--------------------------------|
  | call_received   | ~7.8k | connected / missed / voicemail |
  | lead_created    | ~3k   | open / converted               |
  | appointment_set | ~1.8k | completed / no_show            |
    Outcomes are always consistent with the event type. The schema comment
    doesn't list completed or open, though.
  - Pattern: activity is mostly on weekdays and peaks around 10–12 local time.
    No account goes silent for 6 or more days.

  What looks off

  1. One huge spike: on 2026-06-03, account 6 logged 805 events in a single day
     across all 15 sites, against a normal ~25/day. That one day is most of
     June's monthly bump and the reason Wednesday looks like the busiest
     weekday. It could be a real event or a replay/backfill, and it will badly
     distort any "normal" baseline.
  2. Exact duplicates: 12 pairs share everything except the id, and the ids are
     consecutive (e.g. 1523/1524). It looks like double ingestion.
  3. Missed calls have durations: 1,805 "missed" calls last over 60 seconds,
     some up to 25 minutes. Duration looks random, uniform between 20 and 1500
     seconds whatever the outcome, so I wouldn't trust it as a signal.
  4. NULLs: about 400 events have no outcome (76 of them from account 6) and
     about 310 calls have no duration.
  5. Timezones:
     - Capital City Storage is set to UTC, which is probably a default nobody
       changed.
     - Events late in the evening on the West Coast fall on the next UTC day.
     - Seven accounts are on US Eastern, Central or Pacific time, which change
       for daylight saving in March 2026, inside the data range.

     So any per-day or per-week grouping has to happen in the account's local
     time.
  6. Missing history:
     - Quiet Harbor Spa (account 20) has no events at all.
     - Every account was created in 2025, but events only start on 2026-02-01,
       so there's no early history for building baselines.
     - Some accounts were created only 2–3 months before the data starts.
  7. Uneven site sizes: some single-site accounts are under 1 event a day, and
     account 6's sites average about 7 a week. Small counts like these make "is
     this normal?" noisy.

  I haven't read TICKET.md, as you asked.

● One correction first: I haven't opened TICKET.md. My only reads were
  README.md, PRODUCT_BACKGROUND.md and the two SQL files, and the "is this
  number normal for us?" line is quoted from PRODUCT_BACKGROUND.md. The README
  also gives the ticket's ID (DASH-247), but nothing more.

  Second correction: the "~25/day normal" figure I gave you was wrong. I
  eyeballed it from the next-highest days, which are the busiest days, not
  typical ones. The real baseline is about 13 events per weekday. My "~7/week
  per site" figure was also inflated (explained below).

  Metric

  Events per account per local calendar day. It counts all three event types,
  fills days with no events as 0, and splits weekdays from weekends because
  weekend volume is about a third of weekday volume. "Normal" here means the
  median and spread of that daily count, with the spike day left out. The spike
  is scored two ways: as a ratio to the median, and as a robust z-score, which
  measures distance from the median in units of its spread (MAD) and is less
  thrown off by outliers than a mean-based z-score.

  Q1: account 6, daily counts in local time

  WITH ev AS (
    SELECT date(occurred_at,
                CASE WHEN occurred_at >= '2026-03-08 07:00:00' THEN '-4 hours'
  ELSE '-5 hours' END) AS local_day
    FROM activity_events WHERE account_id = 6
  ),
  cal(d) AS (
    SELECT '2026-02-01' UNION ALL SELECT date(d, '+1 day') FROM cal WHERE d <
  '2026-07-27'
  ),
  daily AS (
    SELECT cal.d, CAST(strftime('%w', cal.d) AS INT) AS dow, COUNT(ev.local_day)
  AS n
    FROM cal LEFT JOIN ev ON ev.local_day = cal.d GROUP BY cal.d
  )
  SELECT * FROM daily;

  The CASE hard-codes New York's daylight-saving switch (2026-03-08). That's
  fine for a one-off check but not for the real implementation. I then
  summarized the output in Python:

  ┌────────────────────────────┬──────┬──────┬────────┬─────┬─────┬─────┐
  │ Slice (spike day excluded) │ Days │ Mean │ Median │ p95 │ Max │ MAD │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────┼──────┼────────┼─────┼─────┼─────┤
  │ All days                   │ 176  │ 10.4 │ 10     │ 22  │ 28  │ 5   │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────┼──────┼────────┼─────┼─────┼─────┤
  │ Weekdays                   │ 125  │ 12.8 │ 13     │ 23  │ 28  │ 4   │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────┼──────┼────────┼─────┼─────┼─────┤
  │ Weekends                   │ 51   │ 4.7  │ 4      │ 8   │ 10  │ 2   │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────┼──────┼────────┼─────┼─────┼─────┤
  │ Wednesdays only            │ 24   │ 12.9 │ 13     │ 23  │ 23  │ 3   │
  └────────────────────────────┴──────┴──────┴────────┴─────┴─────┴─────┘

  Spike day 2026-06-03 (a Wednesday): 805 events. That's 62× the weekday median,
  with a robust z of about 133. The next-highest day in six months is 28.

  Q2: account 6, per location on the spike day

  WITH ev AS (
    SELECT location,
           date(occurred_at, CASE WHEN occurred_at >= '2026-03-08 07:00:00' THEN
  '-4 hours' ELSE '-5 hours' END) AS d
    FROM activity_events WHERE account_id = 6
  ),
  per_loc AS (
    SELECT location,
           SUM(d = '2026-06-03') AS spike_n,
           SUM(d <> '2026-06-03' AND strftime('%w', d) BETWEEN '1' AND '5') *
  1.0 / 125 AS weekday_avg
    FROM ev GROUP BY location
  )
  SELECT location, spike_n, ROUND(weekday_avg, 2) AS weekday_avg, ROUND(spike_n
  / weekday_avg, 0) AS ratio
  FROM per_loc ORDER BY location;

  Every one of the 15 sites spikes at once. Each site normally has about 0.7–1.0
  events per weekday and had 42–69 that day, a jump of 44× to 81×. So a normal
  site is about 4–5 events per weekday week. My earlier "~7/week" averaged only
  the weeks that had events, and the spike week was included.

  The read

  - This looks like a data artifact, not real activity. A site averaging under 1
    event a day doesn't take 60 in one day, let alone all 15 sites on the same
    day, with the same event mix and spread over the whole day.
  - It breaks a mean-based baseline. Leaving the day in pushes account 6's daily
    mean from 10.4 to about 14.9, a 43% jump from one row-day.
  - It decides the design questions for the ticket. A "normal for us?" baseline
    should:
    - be robust to outliers (median/MAD or a trimmed mean, not mean/stddev),
    - be computed per weekday/weekend, or per day of week,
    - group by local time,
    - be computed per location as well as per account.

    Per-location counts under 1 a day are mostly zeros and ones, so daily
    per-site comparisons will be noisy. Weekly windows, or flagging only above a
    minimum volume, are probably needed.
  - Whether to exclude or flag the spike is a spec question. Raw totals should
    probably still count it, but it should be kept out of the baseline. That's
    worth checking against the ticket.

  The query files are at <scratchpad>/q1.sql and
  <scratchpad>/q2.sql, and the SQLite DB is
  <scratchpad>/relay.db.

  the audit, then the corrections, then my read of the ticket.

  The skill

  It's at relay-analytics/.claude/skills/verify/SKILL.md. It triggers whenever I
  report counts, rates, baselines, date ranges, spikes, nulls or duplicates, or
  describe how the data is shaped, and you can call it as /verify. For each
  claim it shows the query and the raw output, re-runs anything I eyeballed,
  labels reasoning as inferred, and ends with a list of corrections. It also
  lists the traps I keep falling into: grouping by UTC instead of local time,
  averaging only days that had events, wrong denominators, the spike and the
  duplicates, and partial periods.

  Nothing is committed: relay-analytics/ is not a git repo yet, and I'll run git
  init and commit once you've picked the final folder and name. If /verify
  doesn't show up as a command, restart Claude Code from that folder.

  Corrections:
  - Daylight saving time affects 17 accounts, not 7, so getting it right matters
    for nearly every account.
  - The per-site figure for account 6 counts weekdays only.
  - The UTC vs local mismatch is real but touches only 67 events. It matters
    mainly at week boundaries.

  The scripts are <scratchpad>/weekly.py and
  <scratchpad>/recent.py. All weeks below run
  Monday to Sunday in each account's local time, with empty weeks filled as 0.

  The ticket (DASH-247) and "normal"

  The ask: on Monday morning, a customer admin should see whether this week is
  good, bad or typical, including for each location. Alerting and ML/forecasting
  are out of scope.

  What the data supports
  - The data ends exactly on a Monday morning. The last event is on Monday
    2026-07-27 local time, so "this week" means the last complete week, 07-20 to
    07-26. There are 25 complete weeks; the first and last weeks are partial
    and should be left out.
  - Totals are flat. The all-account weekly total is 421–503 in every week
    except the spike week (1,270). There's no trend and no dip around Memorial
    Day or July 4. My guess (inferred) is that the data is synthetic and has no
    seasonality. Either way, a long window of the account's own history works as
    a baseline, and with only 6 months there's no way to compare against the
    same week last year.
  - Account-level comparisons are stable. Weekly variation is 16–26% for most
    accounts, so "this week vs your typical week" is a meaningful comparison at
    the account level.

  What can go wrong
  1. The spike breaks any mean-based baseline. For account 6, the 8-week
     trailing mean is 169–171 events/week against a median of 66–72. Because a
     trailing window keeps the spike for 8 weeks, a mean-based baseline would
     call every week "far below normal" for two months. Use a median, or exclude
     flagged days from the baseline but keep them in the raw totals.
  2. Locations are tiny, and that's the core problem for "which location needs 
     attention?"
     - Site-level weekly medians run from 4 to 11, and 24% of site-weeks have
       fewer than 5 events.
     - The biggest 4-week shifts are −32% to +62%, all on counts of 5–12. That's
       what random noise looks like at these sizes (inferred, from Poisson
       reasoning).
     - Rules based on percentage change will flag noise.
     - A median/MAD z-score also over-flags at these sizes, because the MAD
       collapses to 1: account 12 Site F scores z = 3.4 on 11 events vs 6, and
       account 15 Site C scores 4.7 on 12 vs 5.
     - So show a typical range from the location's own history, with a
       minimum-volume rule, rather than a hard verdict.
     - I found no location with a clear, sustained change in volume. If the data
       hides a "needs attention" site, it isn't in volume, or not yet. I
       haven't checked rates per location.
  3. More activity isn't automatically good. Rising calls with a rising
     missed-call rate is bad, so "good or bad" probably needs rate metrics:
     missed calls, no-shows, lead conversion. But rates swing wildly on small
     accounts: account 16's weekly missed-call rate ranges from 0% to 100% on a
     median of 4 calls a week, while account 6's ranges from 14% to 32%. Rates
     need a minimum denominator.
  4. Outcome quirks:
     - The 398 null outcomes need a rule: exclude them, or show them as
       "unknown".
     - Inferred, not checked yet: a lead marked open may convert later, so
       recent weeks would look worse on conversion.
     - Duration can't be used as a metric.
  5. Cleaning before counting: remove the 12 duplicate pairs, and treat Capital
     City Storage's UTC timezone as suspicious and worth confirming.
  6. Too little history: account 20 has no events, so the dashboard needs a "not
     enough history yet" state. Every account in the seed happens to have 25
     weeks, but real accounts won't, so set a minimum number of weeks.
  7. Compare each location to its own history, not to its sibling locations.
     Sites within an account differ in size (weekly medians range 4–11), so a
     site-vs-site ranking just reflects size. Rank locations by how far they are
     from their own normal.
  8. Keep it explainable. With ML ruled out, the right output is something like
     "Site C: 3 calls this week, typical is 5–9", plus a clear rule for when
     that counts as unusual.

  Questions for the spec:
  - Should the spike be shown, flagged or excluded from the baseline?
  - Which metrics define "good" (volume vs rates)?
  - What minimum volume is needed before we label a location?

● Checking your three questions against the data, and testing your rules on the

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Your three questions

  1. Are account 6's 76 null outcomes from the spike day? Mostly not: 21 are on
  the spike day and 55 are spread over other days. The null share is 2.9% for
  account 6 overall, 3.0% for account 6 excluding the spike day, and 3.2% for
  every other account. Nulls look like an even background rate. They are not a
  side effect of the spike.

  2. Near-duplicates within a few seconds? There are very few, and they don't
  look like duplicates. I compared each event with the next one of the same
  account, location and event type:

  ┌───────────────────────────┬────────┐
  │            Gap            │ Pairs  │
  ├───────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ 0s (the exact duplicates) │ 12     │
  ├───────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ 1–5s                      │ 2      │
  ├───────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ 6–60s                     │ 23     │
  ├───────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ 61–300s                   │ 106    │
  ├───────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ >300s                     │ 12,276 │
  └───────────────────────────┴────────┘

  - 16 of the 25 pairs under a minute fall on the spike day, where events are
    packed closely anyway.
  - Almost all of them differ in duration or outcome. For example, ids 8972/8973
    are 4 seconds apart: one is connected and lasts 644s, the other is missed
    and lasts 312s.
  - The 12 exact pairs are identical in every column except id.

  I'd remove only exact duplicates and skip fuzzy matching on time. Seven more
  pairs share the same second but differ in location or event type; six of those
  are on the spike day, consistent with how dense it is (inferred).

  3. Locations that start late or go quiet?
  - Late starts: none. Every location's first event is on or before 2026-02-07.
  - Going quiet at the end: account 15 Site B and account 11 Site A have no
    events after Wed 07-22, which is 5 silent days before the data ends. Both
    had 5–6 day gaps earlier in the year, so it isn't unprecedented. 15 Site B
    shows 0 calls in the 07-20 week against a baseline median of 5. 11 Site A
    had a normal week (5 calls, median 4.5), so a weekly view won't catch it
    until next week.
  - A real outage in the history: account 6 Site G had no events for 15 days, 
    04-08 to 04-23, at a site that averages about 0.9 events a day. It had zero
    events in the 04-13 week.
  - Sparse event types: 53 location × event-type combinations have 3 or more
    zero weeks in a row, mostly appointments.

  Pushback

  I tested your rules on the 07-20 week, with the baseline being the 8 previous
  weeks (05-25 to 07-13, which includes the spike week).

  1. Location × event type is too fine-grained to flag on. The baseline medians
     per location are: calls 4 a week, leads 1.5, appointments 1. With a minimum
     of 5, 188 of 207 location × type cells would show "too little activity to 
     tell", and with a minimum of 3 it's still 146. The dashboard would mostly
     show that message. With all event types summed per location, only 8 of 69
     are below 5 and none are below 3.
     - My suggestion: flag at the location total level, and at account level per
       event type. Show the per-type numbers for each location as context,
       without a flag.
  2. Apply the minimum volume to the baseline, not to this week's count. If it
     gates this week's count, a drop to zero can never be flagged, and a drop to
     zero is the most useful signal. Site G in the 04-13 week shows the
     difference:
     - Calls: 0 against a median of 3, so even gated on the baseline it's "too
       little to tell" when split by type.
     - All events summed: 0 against a median of 5.5, so the outage is flagged.
  3. The spike is only harmless if the typical range trims extremes. The median
     handles it: account 6 Site A calls have a baseline of [2, 2, 2, 3, 5, 5, 5,
     26] and a median of 4. A min–max range, though, would run from 2 to 26 for
     8 weeks. Define the range as the 2nd to 7th of the 8 sorted values; then
     "no special rule" holds. With that range, 4 cells get flagged this week,
     including 15 Site B (0 calls, range 3–6).
  4. The as-of date must be the same for the whole dataset. It can't come from
     each account's or location's own latest event, or a quiet location silently
     uses an older week and looks fine. On "last day minus 7" vs complete
     Mon–Sun weeks: the data ends on a Monday, which suits complete weeks for
     v1. A rolling window can come later as you said.
  5. "Distance from normal" needs a unit. A percentage puts tiny sites at the
     top (going from 2 to 0 is −100%), while an absolute difference puts big
     sites at the top. My suggestion: flagged locations first, then by how far
     outside the range they are, scaled by the location's own median, using the
     location total.
  6. Missed-call rate: with a median of 4 calls per location per week, a weekly
     rate per location is mostly noise. Do it per account, or over a 4-week
     window per location.

  I agree with the rest: the spike stays in raw totals, duplicates are removed
  at query time on exact match, and nulls count for volume but not rates.

● PLAN.md is written at relay-analytics/PLAN.md. It covers interpretation, 12
  decisions each with the alternatives we weighed, 8 questions for product, data
  handling, the API, the UI, the testing approach and out-of-scope, with "Where
  would an LLM fit?" left empty for you. Nothing is committed yet, since the
  folder still isn't a git repo.

  You need to make one decision before the rule tests are written. With the
  middle-6 range, about 30% of eligible locations come out flagged each week. At
  the latest data that's 18 of 61, and most of them are only 1–2 events outside
  the range. I think a Monday list with a third of the sites flagged doesn't
  work "at a glance". The plan records your rule as decided and lists this as an
  open decision with three options:
  - (a) Keep the rule as it is.
  - (b) Also require a minimum distance outside the range. With a threshold of
    0.3, the latest week flags 3 locations instead of 18, and the 04-13 week
    flags 7. It still catches the Site G outage.
  - (c) Widen the range, which lets the spike back in.

  I recommend (b).

  Two other choices are mine and worth a look:
  - Stack. I proposed Postgres, FastAPI, Alembic and React/Vite. Postgres is
    there mainly because it handles timezones and daylight saving natively. Swap
    it if the take-home prompt says otherwise.
  - Location history starts on the first Monday on or after a location's first
    event, so a partial first week never gets into a baseline.

  The seed answers the tests will check are in section 7. They come from a
  separate reference script built on SQLite and Python's timezone library, not
  from the app code. The plan commits that script under analysis/ so every value
  can be traced back.

● I've updated PLAN.md and committed it on its own as e695143 on main, in a
  fresh repo at relay-analytics/. Nothing else is committed. The starter files,
  docs/ and the /verify skill are still untracked and will go in with the
  skeleton phase.

  What changed in the plan:
  - Option (b) is decided (D13): a week is flagged only when it is clearly
    outside its range, with the threshold kept as a constant, MIN_FLAG_DISTANCE 
    = 0.3.
    - What 0.3 means: this week is past the edge of its usual range by at least
      30% of a normal week. For a location whose typical week is 10 events,
      that's 3 or more beyond the edge. Combined with the minimum baseline
      median of 5, it takes at least 2 events beyond the edge to raise a flag,
      so one stray event never does.
    - A week outside the range by less than that shows as "slightly above" or
      "slightly below", with the range shown and no flag.
    - The comparison uses exact arithmetic, so a distance of exactly 0.3 counts
      as flagged, and there's a test for it.
  - The URL holds the selection (D14): /?account=15&as_of=…, so it survives a
    reload and works with back/forward. An unknown account in the URL shows a
    "not found" state.
  - One command runs everything (D15): docker compose up --build starts the
    database, backend and frontend. The backend runs migrations and loads the
    seed only if the database is empty.
  - New section 8, implementation order:
    a. Skeleton.
    b. Rules and their tests.
    c. Data layer, then the seed known-answer tests.
    d. Endpoint, then a minimal UI with URL state.
    e. Refinement: per-type context, account totals and frontend tests. This
       phase goes first if time runs short.
    f. README.

  Commits:
  ace6c9a Add schema and index migrations and idempotent seed loader
  4bb77d0 Add phase 1 test harness and failing schema/seed tests   ← tests 
  failing, before the code
  d2e3f08 Add starter dataset, repo config and verify skill
  e695143 Add implementation plan for DASH-247

  What's in it:
  - Docker Compose: a Postgres 16 db service, and a backend service that runs
    migrations, then the seed load, then exits. Phase 4 adds the API server to
    it.
  - Two migrations:
    - The schema, matching schema.sql exactly: plain integer ids with no
      auto-numbering, and timestamps stored as UTC without a timezone.
    - The two indexes from the plan.
  - Seed loader: python -m relay.seed loads the file unchanged, in one
    transaction, only into an empty database, and takes a table lock so two
    starts at once can't double-load.
  - Makefile: make up / test / migrate / seed / psql. make test runs inside the
    container against a separate relay_test database that is recreated for each
    test.

  The tests check:
  - column types, lengths and nullability against schema.sql;
  - that ids have no sequence;
  - the foreign key and the indexes, and that downgrading removes everything;
  - that seed.sql is byte-for-byte the starter file (sha256);
  - the counts: 20 accounts and 12,626 events, before any duplicate removal;
  - that loading twice changes nothing;
  - four rows copied from the file, including ones with NULLs;
  - that timestamps don't shift when the database session runs in another
    timezone.

  To check that the tests really bite, I temporarily switched auto-numbering
  back on for an id: the sequence test failed, and I reverted it.

  Choices I made along the way:
  - schema.sql and seed.sql moved into data/, unchanged. The /verify skill now
    points to the new paths.
  - Port 8000 on your machine is taken by another container, so
    phase 4 will expose the backend on a different port. The database uses 5432,
    which you can change with DB_PORT.

  Decision for you: README.md and docs/ are still untracked. The README gets
  rewritten in phase 6. The starter README says docs/ doesn't have to be carried
  over, but PLAN.md refers to the ticket. Should I commit docs/ or leave it
  out?

  Verify: 20 accounts and 12,626 events come from grep -c on the seed's INSERT
  lines, the hash from sha256sum, and the sample rows are copied from the file.
  The tests assert those values.

      191  ## 10. Where would an LLM fit?
      192  
      193 +**Not in v1.**                                                
      194 +- **The answer is already one deterministic line**, e.g. "Site
          + B: 2 events this week, typical 4–12". The feature only works 
          +if admins trust that line. An LLM adds cost, latency and the r
          +isk of a wrong number, for little gain.                       
      195 +- **It can't explain why a site dropped.** We only store count
          +s: no call notes, no staffing data, no marketing calendar. Any
          + "because…" it wrote would be a guess presented as an insight.
      196 +                                                              
      197 +**Later, if the need shows up:**                              
      198 +- **A Monday digest for large multi-location accounts**: a sho
          +rt paragraph on top of the list.                              
      199 +  - It's built only from the computed weekly summary, never fr
          +om raw data.                                                  
      200 +  - Every number in the generated text is checked against the 
          +summary; if any doesn't match, the deterministic template is s
          +hown instead.                                                 
      201 +  - It's generated once per account per week and cached, so co
          +st and latency don't touch page loads.                        
      202 +- **Classifying call reasons**, if Relay ever stores call note
          +s or transcripts. That would give a real "why" behind a drop. 
          +Today there's no such data.

● Phase 2 is done: two commits, with the tests committed before the code. All 40
  tests pass: the 29 new rule tests plus the 11 from phase 1.

  452aaa0 Implement the weekly normality rule and ranking
  098a200 Add failing rule tests and threshold constants    ← 28 of 29 failing 
  on stubs

  What's in it:
  - relay/thresholds.py: the four constants: BASELINE_WEEKS = 8, RANGE_TRIM = 1,
    MIN_BASELINE_MEDIAN = 5, MIN_FLAG_DISTANCE = 3/10.
  - relay/normality.py: plain functions with no database access.
    - assess(location, count, baseline, limits) returns the status, median,
      typical range and distance for one location's week.
    - rank(assessments) puts flagged locations first, then slightly outside
      (each by distance, largest first), then typical, then too little activity,
      then insufficient history. Ties go by location name.
    - All arithmetic uses exact fractions, so a distance of exactly 0.3 counts
      as flagged.

  The 29 rule tests use small made-up baselines, with every expected value
  worked out by hand in a comment. They cover:
  - the median of an even number of weeks, the middle-6 range (also with ties),
    and that one 200-event week doesn't move either;
  - that only the last 8 weeks count;
  - typical on and inside the range edges, slightly below, slightly above at 2/7
    ≈ 0.286, and flagged at exactly 3/10 in both directions;
  - the drop-to-zero case: a median of 5 with this week at 0 is flagged, while a
    median of 4.5 is "too little activity", even when this week jumps to 40;
  - an all-zero baseline, which doesn't divide by zero;
  - fewer than 8 weeks of history;
  - each threshold changed on its own, which proves none of them is hard-coded,
    and an invalid trim setting raising an error;
  - the order across all seven statuses, the Site G / Site I style tie, and that
    the input order doesn't matter.

  Checking that the tests catch real mistakes: I injected four bugs one at a
  time, and each made tests fail:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────┐
  │                       Injected bug                       │   Failing    │
  │                                                          │    tests     │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Flag boundary >= changed to >                            │ 3            │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Minimum volume checked on this week instead of the       │ 4            │
  │ baseline                                                 │              │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Range off by one at the top                              │ 9            │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ No tie-break by name                                     │ 2            │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────┘

  Design choices worth a look:
  - assess expects its baseline to be the weekly counts before this week, oldest
    first, with empty weeks as 0 and only weeks since the location's history
    started. Producing exactly that is phase 3's job.
  - "Too little activity" still returns the median and range, so the UI can say
    "typically under 5 a week". Only the distance is left empty.
  - The exact-0.3 tests prove the boundary is inclusive. They don't prove
    fractions are needed: by my reasoning (inferred), plain floating-point
    division would give the same answers for these simple ratios. I kept
    fractions anyway because they cost nothing and remove the question.

  Phase 3 is next: the aggregation queries (removing duplicates, local-week
  bucketing, zero-filled weeks, history start), the SQL tests on synthetic rows,
  and the seed known-answer tests, with the reference script committed under
  analysis/.

● Phase 3 is done. All 85 tests pass, and every seed answer from the app matches

  a94bc60 Implement dedup view, local weekly aggregation and account summary
  7068701 Fix test helper creating exact duplicates for n > 1
  cc7946e Add reference calculation and failing aggregation tests   ← 45 
  failing, before the code

  What's in it:
  - analysis/reference.py: a second implementation of the whole rule that shares
    no code with the app: it uses SQLite, Python's zoneinfo and its own median,
    range and ranking code. Its printed output is where the seed test values
    come from; the tests never run it.
  - Migration 0003: a view, activity_events_dedup, that counts rows identical in
    every column except id once. It counts rows with NULLs once too, which
    matters because some of the seed duplicates have NULL outcomes.
  - relay/weekly.py:
    - target_week() returns the last complete Mon–Sun week before the as-of
      date.
    - default_as_of() returns the UTC date of the latest event.
    - location_weeks() runs one SQL query that converts to local time with
      Postgres's timezone support, groups by Mon–Sun week, fills empty weeks
      with 0 across the 8 baseline weeks plus the target week, and starts a
      location's history on the first Monday on or after its first event.
  - relay/summary.py: weekly_summary() combines the aggregation, the rule and
    the ranking for one account. It returns None for an unknown account.

  Tests:

  File: test_weekly.py
  Tests: 21
  What they cover: Sunday 23:30 vs Monday 00:30 local; a UTC Monday that is
  still
  Sunday locally; both sides of the 2026-03-08 daylight-saving switch; Phoenix
  and UTC accounts; exact duplicates (with and without NULLs); near-duplicates
  and same-second rows kept; zero-filled weeks; the history-start rule;
  locations first seen during or after the target week; the 9-week window;
  per-type counts; the same location name in two accounts
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: test_seed_known_answers.py
  Tests: 15
  What they cover: 12,626 → 12,614 rows; the account 6 spike week (880 events);
  history starts (51 locations on 02-02, 18 on 02-09); default dates; status
  counts at four as-of dates; the flagged sets; the full ranked lists for
  account 15 (latest week) and account 6 (the Site G outage week, and the
  latest week with the spike inside its baseline); account 20 empty

  A mistake on my side, caught by the tests: my test helper's n=2 inserted
  identical rows, so the duplicate removal correctly counted them as one. The
  fix went into its own commit, and no expected values changed.

  Checking that the tests catch real mistakes: I injected five bugs one at a
  time, and each one made tests fail:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────┐
  │                  Injected bug                   │ Failing tests │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ UTC date instead of local date                  │ 5             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ Reading the raw table (no duplicate removal)    │ 3             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ Duplicate removal keyed on id (removes nothing) │ 4             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ Partial first week counted as history           │ 6             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ No zero-fill                                    │ 16            │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────┘

  I also ran the full suite with the database session set to Los Angeles time:
  85 pass, so session settings can't shift the results.

  One edge I found and haven't fixed: the default as-of date is the UTC date of
  the latest event (plan D2). If data arrives up to Sunday evening Pacific time,
  the UTC date is already Monday. The default would then pick a week that
  hasn't ended locally for US accounts. The seed isn't affected: its last event
  is Monday 22:20 UTC, so every account's previous week is complete. The fix I'd
  suggest is to default to "today" in production and keep latest-event only for
  the seed. Do you want that in phase 4, or noted in the README?

  Verify: the 12,614 rows, the 880 spike-week events, the history starts, the
  four sets of status counts, the flagged sets and the full ranked lists all
  come from analysis/reference.py output, and test_seed_known_answers.py asserts
  them against the app. The per-injected-bug failure counts are the raw pytest
  output. The as-of edge case is reasoning (inferred), not something I tested.

  Phase 4 is next: the endpoint with API tests, then the minimal UI with URL
  state and the full Docker Compose setup.

● Here's the draft CLAUDE.md. Nothing is committed yet: both files are only
  changed on disk.

  # CLAUDE.md

  DASH-247: show customers whether last week's activity per location is normal
  for them. The ticket is quoted in README.md.

  ## Running things

  ```bash
  make up                     # docker compose up --build: db, migrations, seed
  (loaded once)
  make test                   # backend tests in the container, against
  throwaway databases
  make test ARGS="-q tests/test_weekly.py"
  make migrate | make seed | make psql | make down
  python3 -I analysis/reference.py data/schema.sql data/seed.sql   # independent
  reference answers
  ```

  Ports: `DB_PORT` (default 5432), `BACKEND_PORT` (default 8000).

  ## Where things live

  - `PLAN.md`: interpretation, decisions D1–D15 with alternatives, product
  assumptions, implementation order (section 8). Progress is in `git log`.
  - `backend/relay/thresholds.py`: the tunable constants. Never put threshold
  literals in SQL or other code.
  - `backend/relay/normality.py`: the rule and ranking, pure functions in exact
  arithmetic (`Fraction`).
  - `backend/relay/weekly.py`: the aggregation SQL. `backend/relay/summary.py`:
  wires aggregation and rule together for one account.
  - `analysis/reference.py`: independent implementation (SQLite + `zoneinfo`)
  that shares no code with the backend.
  - `docs/` is gitignored; it's context, not part of the repo.

  ## Data rules

  - `data/seed.sql` is used as-is. Never edit, regenerate or extend it; a test
  checks its hash.
  - Count from the `activity_events_dedup` view: exact duplicates (equal on
  every column except `id`, NULLs included) count once. Near-duplicates are
  kept.
  - Local time comes from each account's IANA `timezone` (Postgres `AT TIME
  ZONE`, `zoneinfo` in analysis). Never use fixed offsets. Weeks are Mon–Sun in
  local time.
  - The as-of date is global: by default the UTC date of the latest event in the
  whole dataset, never per account or location. The target week is the last
  complete week before it.
  - A location's history starts on the first Monday on or after its first event.
  Weeks inside the history with no events are 0; weeks before it are no
  history.
  - Status is computed on location totals; per-type counts are context only.
  NULL outcomes count toward volume. `duration_seconds` is not used.
  - The account 6 spike (2026-06-03) stays in the raw totals, with no special
  handling.

  ## Working rules

  - **TDD.** Commit the failing tests before the code that makes them pass.
  - **Expected values** come from hand-worked synthetic cases (arithmetic in a
  comment) or from `analysis/reference.py` output, never from running the app
  code. If the code disagrees, investigate; never change a test to match the
  code.
  - **Testing effort** goes to the rules and the aggregation. Schema, API and
  frontend tests stay thin.
  - **Check that new tests bite:** inject the bug they target, confirm a
  failure, restore.
  - **Any number or claim about the data** comes with the query that produced it
  and its raw output. Run `/verify` and label inferences as inferred.
  - **`PLAN.md` is not edited unless the user says so.** Deviations and known
  limitations go in README.md.

  I also added the as-of edge case to README.md, in a new section called
  "Deviations from PLAN.md and known limitations". The section also gives
  somewhere to record future deviations, as the last working rule says.

  There's one thing in the draft you didn't ask for: the "check that new tests
  bite" rule. It's what we've done in every phase, but drop it if you don't want
  it as a standing rule. I left out stack conventions, as you said.

  Once you're happy with it, I'll commit CLAUDE.md and the README note together.

  bb848d5 Add minimal UI with URL state and run everything from compose

  578efb4 Add failing API contract tests and web dependencies   ← 8 of 9 
  failing, before the code

  Endpoint:
  - GET /api/accounts lists the accounts with their location counts.
  - GET /api/accounts/{id}/weekly-summary?as_of= returns the ranked weekly
    summary.
  - Each route is a thin layer over relay.summary, with a Pydantic response
    model.
  - An unknown account gets a 404 and a malformed date gets a 422.
  - The response returns the threshold values that were actually used.
  - Per-type counts and account totals are left out until phase 5, as planned.

  API tests (9, thin):
  - They check the accounts list, the summary for account 15, an explicit as-of
    date for account 6 (Site G, then Site I), the null fields for "too little
    activity" and "not enough history", the empty account, 404 and 422.
  - Expected values come from the reference output and the first SQLite profile.
  - Three bugs I injected (no 404, as-of ignored, flagged always false) each
    made a test fail.

  New dependencies:
  - Backend: fastapi and uvicorn, the planned stack.
  - Backend tests: httpx, which FastAPI's test client needs.
  - Frontend: React, Vite with its React plugin, and TypeScript.

  UI (React + TypeScript strict + Vite, served by nginx, which forwards /api to 
  the backend):
  - The account and as-of date live only in the URL:
    /?account=6&as_of=2026-04-20.
  - Fetching lives in hooks, and every fetch handles loading, error and empty
    states.
  - The list shows the API's order, with status as text plus ▲/▼.
  - Flagged rows are highlighted. "Slightly above/below" rows show the range
    with no flag.

  What I checked in Chrome:
  - Account 15 shows the right three rows.
  - Changing the account and date through the controls updates the URL. A reload
    keeps the selection and shows Site G (0 events, typical 2–7), then Site I,
    then Site N, matching the reference.
  - An unknown account shows "Account not found."
  - An invalid date shows the date error.
  - Account 20 shows "No activity recorded…".
  - An early as-of date shows "Not enough history yet · needs 8 full weeks of
    history".

  What I couldn't check:
  - The layout. The screenshot call timed out, so I never saw the rendered
    styling, the dark mode or the narrow-screen layout. The checks above read
    the page structure, not pixels.
  - The back button. The browser tool's history navigation failed, so
    back/forward is untested. The code listens for popstate.

  Other notes:
  - On this machine I ran the backend with BACKEND_PORT=8001, since 8000 is
    taken here. The default stays 8000, and the UI is on FRONTEND_PORT,
    default 5173.
  - The line in your CLAUDE.md conventions still reads
    relay/[weekly.py](http://weekly.py). I committed it as you wrote it; tell me
    if you want it fixed.

  Verify: the API test values come from analysis/reference.py output (week

❯ start phase 5                                                                 

● Phase 5 has three parts: per-type counts per location, account totals, and
  frontend tests. One definition comes first, because the plan doesn't pin it
  down. Account totals are the sums of the location numbers. For each event
  type:
  - This week's count is the sum of the locations' counts for this week, so the
    header always adds up to the rows below it.
  - The baseline sums each location's weeks from its own history start, and it
    begins at the earliest history start among the account's locations.
  - Median and typical range use the same assess() function and thresholds as
    the locations, so there's no new logic. Totals are context only and never
    flagged.

  I'm extending the reference script with this, independently of the app.

      49  ## Deviations from PLAN.md and known limitations
      50  
      51 +- **Account totals (not defined in PLAN.md).** The header's per
         +-type totals are the sums of the location numbers. This week's 
         +count is the sum of the locations' counts, so it always adds up
         + to the rows. The baseline sums each location's weeks from its 
         +own history start, beginning at the account's earliest location
         + history. Median and typical range use the same rule and thresh
         +olds as locations. Totals are context only and never flagged.  
      52 +                                                               
      53  - **Default as-of uses the UTC date of the latest event (D2).**
           If data arrives up to a Sunday evening in a US timezone, the U
          TC date is already Monday, so the default picks a week that has
          n't ended locally. The seed isn't affected: its last event is M
          onday 2026-07-27 22:20 UTC. In production the default should be
           today's date.

  Account totals: the plan didn't define these, so I picked a definition and
  recorded it in the README.
  - This week's count is the sum of the location counts, so the header always
    matches the rows below it.
  - The baseline adds up each location's weeks from its own history start.
  - The median and typical range come from the same assess() rule and thresholds
    as the locations.
  - They're context only and never flagged.
  - The reference script computes them independently.

  Backend tests:
  - Four synthetic tests with the arithmetic in comments: the sum, a new
    location's partial first week left out of the baseline, not enough history,
    and an account with no events.
  - Seed known answers for account 15 at the latest week, account 6 in the week
    of 04-13, and account 1 with too little history.
  - One check that the totals add up to the rows for every account.
  - Three injected bugs, and each made tests fail.

  One thing the seed shows: in the Site G outage week (04-13), account 6 as a
  whole logged 33 calls against a typical 43–54. That dip is visible in the
  header but not flagged, by design. An account-level flag could be a later
  idea.

  UI:
  - Each row gains a line: "calls 7 · leads 3 · appointments 2".
  - Under the heading there's a line of account totals, e.g. "Calls 9 (typical
    11–19)". Without a baseline it shows only the count.

  Frontend tests (16, Vitest + Testing Library, run with make test-frontend in a
  Node container):
  - They cover the API's order being kept, the icon plus text for flagged rows,
    slightly-outside rows showing their range without a flag, and the two
    messages for rows that can't be assessed.
  - They also cover per-type counts, account totals, and the week/baseline line.
  - On the URL: reading the account and as-of date from it, writing to it while
    keeping the as-of date, the "Latest data" button, and back/forward. That
    last one also covers the gap from phase 4, where the browser tool couldn't
    test the back button.
  - They check loading, the 404, 422 and 500 messages, and the no-activity
    state.
  - Three injected bugs (the UI re-sorting rows, "Latest data" not clearing the
    date, popstate ignored) each made tests fail.

  Fourteen of the 16 cover phase-4 behaviour that was built before the tests
  existed. Those were written after the code, not test-first.

  New dev dependencies:
  - vitest: the test runner.
  - jsdom: a browser DOM for the tests.
  - @testing-library/react: user-facing queries, as your conventions require.
  - @testing-library/dom: required by the React package.

  I added make test-frontend to CLAUDE.md's run section. I haven't looked at the
  styling myself (no screenshot this time either), so the visual review is all
  yours.

  Verify:
  - The account-totals expected values come from the reference script output.
    For example, account 15's counts are 9 / 6 / 5, which adds up to its
    location counts 12 + 2 + 6 = 20.
  - Account 6 in the week of 04-13: 33 calls against 43–54. Its counts are 33 +
    16 + 6 = 55, which matches the sum of the 15 location counts from the
    earlier reference run.
  - The live API after the rebuild returns those same totals.
  - The frontend test data is hand-made, since the UI only displays what the API
    returns.

  Phase 6 (the README wrap-up) is what's left after your review.

  Contrast (WCAG 2.2):
  - Text already passed SC 1.4.3 (4.5:1 needed) in both themes, at 5.8:1 or
    better, so I left it alone.
  - Input, select and button borders failed SC 1.4.11 at 1.2–1.7:1 (3:1 needed).
    They now have their own border colour: 4.6–4.8:1 in light mode and
    4.1–5.0:1 in dark mode.
  - The light-mode flag border failed at 2.86:1 against the flagged background.
    Darkened, it's now 4.51:1.
  - Focus: keyboard focus now shows an explicit outline at 6:1 or better.
  - One border still measures below 3:1: the thin edge around each location
    card. It isn't interactive and the text carries the content, so that
    criterion doesn't apply to it.

  I measured these colour ratios with a script; I haven't looked at the rendered
  page.

  Attention line: when nothing is flagged, a line under the week heading says
  "No locations need attention this week." If no location could be assessed at
  all (only "too little activity" or "not enough history"), it says "Not enough
  activity to assess any location this week." instead, so it never claims all is
  well without evidence. You didn't ask for that second wording; drop it if you
  don't want it. The line is hidden when something is flagged. At the latest
  week it shows for accounts 1–5, for example.

  Your conventions say the frontend never computes. This line only checks
  whether any row has flagged: true, which I count as display logic. If you'd
  rather the API send that, it's a small change.

  As-of hint: under the controls it now says "Shows the last complete week
  (Mon–Sun) before this date.", and screen readers announce it with the date
  field.

  New tests (written first): 3 failed before the code: the attention line in
  both versions and the as-of hint. A fourth checks that no line appears when
  something is flagged; it passed from the start and stays as a guard.

  README "To revisit with product":
  - Unflagged account totals: Pacific Smiles shows appointments 9 vs typical 4–6
    in the week of Jul 13 (as-of 2026-07-20). At the latest week it's 3 vs 4–7,
    which is why I cite the earlier date.
  - The 0.3 threshold: account 6 Site N in the week of Apr 13 had 1 event vs
    typical 3–8. The distance is 2/7 ≈ 0.29, so it shows only as "slightly
    below".
  - Above and below share one highlight until product decides whether more
    activity is good, which depends on the business.

          + account differ too much in size for site-vs-site comparison t
          +o mean anything.                                              
       43 +- **"This week" on a Monday morning means the last complete Mo
          +n–Sun week**, in the account's own timezone.                  
       44 +- **Multi-location is the core case:** one list per account, w
          +ith the locations that need attention first.                  
       45 +- **The answer has to fit one line an admin can trust:** "Site
          + B: 2 events, typical 4–12". With ML out of scope, the rule mu
          +st be explainable.                                            
       46

          -y database

       47 +## Key decisions and trade-offs                               
       48  
       31 -Then open <http://localhost:5173>. The selected account and as
          --of date are in the URL (`/?account=15&as_of=2026-04-20`), so 
          -a reload or a shared link keeps them.                         
       49 +Full list with alternatives: [PLAN.md §2](PLAN.md). Thresholds
          + live in `backend/relay/thresholds.py`.

       53 +| Baseline = **median of the previous 8 weeks**; typical range
          + = **middle 6 of those 8** (D3, D4) | A one-off spike (account
          + 6 logged 880 events in a week that normally has about 70) doe
          +sn't distort the baseline or the range | Only 8 weeks, so no s
          +easonality; slower to notice a gradual trend |                
       54 +| **Status on the location total**, with per-type counts as co
          +ntext only (D5) | Enough volume to say anything; split by type

          +eads and appointments are summed into one number |

          +, not to this week (D6) | A drop to zero gets flagged, which i
          +s the most important Monday signal | Very small sites always r
          +ead "too little activity to tell" |

          +f a normal week beyond the edge (D13) | Roughly 3 flags a week
          + across the seed instead of 18; one stray event never flags | 
          +Near misses show only as "slightly below/above" (see "To revis

       39 -API: `GET /api/accounts` and `GET /api/accounts/{id}/weekly-su
          -mmary?as_of=YYYY-MM-DD` (`as_of` optional, defaults to the lat
          -est data). Interactive docs at `/docs` on the backend port.   
       60 +### Why the typical ranges look wide                          
       61  
       41 -## Data                                                       
       62 +Locations are small. A typical location sees about 6–7 events 
          +a week, and at that size ordinary weeks swing a lot: at locati
          +ons averaging 5–7 events a week, 43% of weeks land more than 3
          +0% from the location's own median, and 1 in 5 more than 50%. T
          +he typical range (usually about 0.7× to 1.4× the median) shows
          + that noise instead of hiding it. That's why a week has to be 
          +clearly outside the range before it's flagged.                
       63  
       43 -`data/seed.sql` is the starter dataset, unchanged (a test chec
          -ks its hash). `data/schema.sql` is the reference DDL that the 
          -Alembic migrations in `backend/migrations` reproduce.         
       64 +## Stack and why                                              
       65  
       45 -## Status                                                     
       66 +- **Postgres:** its `AT TIME ZONE` handles IANA zones and dayl
          +ight saving natively, and local-time bucketing is the riskiest
          + correctness area.                                            
       67 +- **FastAPI + Pydantic:** the response models are the API cont
          +ract.                                                         
       68 +- **SQLAlchemy 2.0 (sync) + Alembic:** for the connection laye
          +r and migrations.                                             
       69 +- **React + TypeScript (strict) + Vite:** served by nginx, whi
          +ch also proxies `/api`, so there's no CORS setup.             
       70 +- **Docker Compose:** runs Postgres, the backend and the front
          +end with one command; tests run in containers too.            
       71  
       47 -Work in progress; see the implementation order in PLAN.md sect
          -ion 8.                                                        
       72 +Notable choices inside the stack:                             
       73  
       49 -## Deviations from PLAN.md and known limitations              
       74 +- **Raw SQL for the aggregation, Alembic's schema operations f
          +or the schema.** The weekly aggregation (removing duplicates, 
          +local-time conversion, `generate_series` to fill empty weeks) 
          +is one readable SQL query with bound parameters (`relay/weekly
          +.py`), tested on synthetic rows. An ORM would hide the part th
          +at most needs to be visible. The schema is defined through Ale
          +mbic migrations, with a duplicate-free view (`activity_events_
          +dedup`).                                                      
       75 +- **SQL buckets the data; pure Python applies the rule.** `rel
          +ay/normality.py` has no database access and uses exact `Fracti
          +on` arithmetic, so a distance of exactly 0.3 is flagged, and e
          +very rule is unit-tested in isolation.                        
       76 +- **The URL is the only UI state.** Account and as-of are read
          + from and written to the query string. Reload, share and back/
          +forward all work, and there is no second copy of the state to 
          +drift.                                                        
       77 +- **The frontend renders and never ranks.** The API returns lo
          +cations already ordered, with an explicit `flagged` field.    
       78 +- **Independent reference calculation.** `analysis/reference.p
          +y` recomputes everything with SQLite and Python's `zoneinfo`, 
          +sharing no code with the app. The seed known-answer tests use 
          +its output, never the app's own.                              
       79  
       51 -- **Account totals (not defined in PLAN.md).** The header's pe
          -r-type totals are the sums of the location numbers. This week'
          -s count is the sum of the locations' counts, so it always adds
          - up to the rows. The baseline sums each location's weeks from 
          -its own history start, beginning at the account's earliest loc
          -ation history. Median and typical range use the same rule and 
          -thresholds as locations. Totals are context only and never fla
          -gged.                                                         
       80 +## Deliberately deferred                                      
       81  
       53 -- **Default as-of uses the UTC date of the latest event (D2).*
          -* If data arrives up to a Sunday evening in a US timezone, the
          - UTC date is already Monday, so the default picks a week that 
          -hasn't ended locally. The seed isn't affected: its last event 
          -is Monday 2026-07-27 22:20 UTC. In production the default shou
          -ld be today's date.                                           
       82 +- **Missed-call rate:** time went to tests instead. It also ne
          +eds a minimum number of calls, and per location probably a 4-w
          +eek window, since a location gets about 4 calls a week.       
       83 +- **Alerting, notifications, ML/forecasting:** out of scope pe
          +r product.                                                    
       84 +- **An LLM in the feature:** the answer is already a determini
          +stic line that admins need to trust. See [PLAN.md §10](PLAN.md
          +) for where one could fit later.                              
       85 +- **Account-level flags, configurable week definitions or a ro
          +lling window, editable thresholds:** each needs a product deci
          +sion first.                                                   
       86 +- **Authentication and tenant isolation, peer benchmarks, fuzz
          +y duplicate matching:** not needed to answer the ticket.      
       87

       95 +                                                              
       96 +## Known limitations                                          
       97 +                                                              
       98 +- **Account totals (not defined in PLAN.md).** The header's pe
          +r-type totals are the sums of the location numbers: this week'
          +s count always adds up to the rows. The baseline sums each loc
          +ation's weeks from its own history start. The median and range
          + use the same rule and thresholds as locations. Totals are con
          +text only and never flagged.                                  
       99 +- **The default as-of is the UTC date of the latest event (D2)
          +.** If data arrives up to a Sunday evening in a US timezone, t
          +he UTC date is already Monday, so the default picks a week tha
          +t hasn't ended locally. The seed isn't affected (its last even
          +t is Monday 2026-07-27 22:20 UTC). In production the default s
          +hould be today's date.

      101  ## To revisit with product
      102  
       57 -- **Account totals are never flagged, even well outside their 
          -range.** Example: Pacific Smiles (account 5), week of Jul 13 (
          -as-of 2026-07-20), appointments 9 vs typical 4–6. This is by d
          -esign for v1, since totals are context only. Whether the accou
          -nt level should get its own marker is a product call.         
      103 +- **Account totals are never flagged, even well outside their 
          +range.** Example: Pacific Smiles (account 5), week of Jul 13 (
          +as-of 2026-07-20), appointments 9 vs typical 4–6. By design fo
          +r v1, since totals are context only.                          
      104  - **`MIN_FLAG_DISTANCE` (0.3) sits close to cases a person wou
           ld call worrying.** Example: account 6 Site N, week of Apr 13 
           (as-of 2026-04-20), 1 event vs typical 3–8. The distance is 2/
           7 ≈ 0.29, so it shows only as "slightly below". Examples like 
           this are what to tune the threshold with.
       59 -- **Above and below typical share the same highlight on purpos
          -e.** Whether more activity is good depends on the business (mo
          -re calls can mean demand or a problem), so the UI stays neutra
          -l until product decides.

          + the UI stays neutral until product decides.

  The README now covers: how to run it (with prerequisites and ports), the
  ticket, how you read it, key decisions with trade-offs (pointing to PLAN.md
  for the rest), why the ranges look wide, the stack and why, notable choices
  inside it, what was deferred, what you'd do with another day, known
  limitations, questions for product, the repo layout, and a one-line test
  command.

  What broke when installing from scratch:
  1. The "ready" cue was wrong. The README said to open the UI once Uvicorn 
     running appeared. At that moment the UI wasn't listening yet, because the
     frontend waits for the backend healthcheck. The README now says to wait for
     the frontend's Configuration complete; ready for start up line; on the
     second clean run the UI answered 200 right at that line.
  2. The tests left root-owned files in the checkout: pytest's cache folder and
     an empty frontend/node_modules. A reviewer couldn't delete the folder
     without sudo, and a root-owned node_modules would block a local npm 
     install. Fixed by turning off pytest's cache and running the frontend tests
     from an image stage with the sources copied in. A second fresh clone
     confirmed: no root-owned files, clean git status, 103 backend and 20
     frontend tests passing, and the README's example URL showing Site G, then
     Site I, then Site N.
  3. Not a repo bug: a second copy of the project can't start while another
     holds port 5432. The README's "ports must be free, or set the variables"
     covers it. Worth knowing: make test leaves the db container running
     afterwards.

  What I couldn't reproduce: a machine that has never pulled the base images
  (Postgres, Python, Node, nginx). I rebuilt with Docker's build cache
  discarded, so pip install and npm ci ran again, but the base images were
  already on disk. Linux only; I haven't tried macOS or Windows.

  Committed files:
  - No docs/, .env, caches, node_modules, dist, databases or logs in any commit
    (I checked the whole history, not only the current tree).
  - No absolute paths and no email address in the files themselves.
  - The only credentials are the local database's relay/relay, in
    docker-compose.yml.

    analysis/reference.py.

       60 -### Why the typical ranges look wide                          
       65 +**Why the typical ranges look wide:** locations are small. A t
          +ypical location sees about 6–7 events a week, and at that size
          + ordinary weeks swing a lot: at locations averaging 5–7 events
          + a week, 43% of weeks land more than 30% from the location's o
          +wn median, and 1 in 5 more than 50%. The typical range (usuall
          +y about 0.7× to 1.4× the median) shows that noise instead of h
          +iding it. That's why a week has to be clearly outside the rang
          +e before it's flagged.

       67  ## Stack and why
       68  
       66 -- **Postgres:** its `AT TIME ZONE` handles IANA zones and dayl
          -ight saving natively, and local-time bucketing is the riskiest
          - correctness area.                                            
       67 -- **FastAPI + Pydantic:** the response models are the API cont
          -ract.                                                         
       68 -- **SQLAlchemy 2.0 (sync) + Alembic:** for the connection laye
          -r and migrations.

          +ract. **SQLAlchemy 2.0 (sync) + Alembic** handle the connectio
          +n layer and migrations.

           ch also proxies `/api`, so there's no CORS setup.

          -end with one command; tests run in containers too.

       98 -- **Account totals (not defined in PLAN.md).** The header's pe

          -s count always adds up to the rows. The baseline sums each loc

          -he UTC date is already Monday, so the default picks a week tha
          -t hasn't ended locally. The seed isn't affected (its last even
          -t is Monday 2026-07-27 22:20 UTC). In production the default s
          -hould be today's date.

✻ Cooked for 20s · done 1:59

● Agent "Code review backend/relay" finished · 3m 9s

● The fresh-context review came back with no high-severity defects. It checked
  the timezone conversion, week buckets, zero-fill, history start, the 9-week
  window, duplicate removal, median, range, the 0.3 boundary, ranking and
  serialization, and found them correct. It reported six findings, two confirmed
  by running code and four from reading. Here they are with my take; nothing is
  changed yet.

  #: 1
  Finding: Account totals are asymmetric for a new location's partial first week

  (summary.py:89, weekly.py:78). It counts in this week's total when it falls
  in the target week, but is dropped when it falls in a baseline week. So this
  week can look inflated against a deflated baseline.
  Severity (reviewer): medium
  My take: Real, and it's my definition. I chose "this week = sum of the rows"
  so
  the header matches the list, but the baseline skips partial first weeks, and
  the docstring and README overstate how consistent that is. No effect on the
  seed at the latest week; it only touches early as-of dates (baseline weeks of
   02-02). The reference script uses the same definition, so the tests agree
  with it; they can't catch it. Fix  options: (a) count partial first weeks in
  the account baseline too, so the totals reflect the account's real events; or
   (b) keep it and correct the docstring and README wording. I lean to (a):
  it's a small SQL/aggregation change, done test-first.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Finding: The default as-of uses the UTC date while weeks use local time. For
  accounts west of UTC it can pick an unfinished local week and falsely flag
  below_typical.
  Severity (reviewer): medium (production), none (seed)
  My take: Agree. It's the limitation you parked, but the reviewer adds 
  something
   new: the README's suggested fix, "today's date", has the same flaw if
  "today" is the UTC date. At minimum the README should say the target week has
   to be complete in every account's timezone, for example today in the
  westernmost account zone, or only weeks that ended at least 24h ago. A
  README-only change.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Finding: Locations first seen after the target week are left out instead of
  showing insufficient_history. With an as-of before any data, a busy account
  looks like "No activity recorded…". location_count in /api/accounts is
  all-time, so it can disagree with the number of rows.
  Severity (reviewer): low–medium
  My take: Mostly disagree: I decided to leave them out (there's a test for it),

  because a location that didn't exist yet at the as-of date shouldn't be
  listed. PLAN §5's insufficient_history was meant for locations that existed.
  Partly agree on the side effects: "No activity recorded for this account yet"
   is accurate for that date but could confuse. A clearer message for an as-of
  before the account's first activity would be cheap. The location_count
  mismatch is harmless (it only feeds the picker).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Finding: ?as_of=0001-01-01 (any date up to about 0001-03) returns a 500 
  instead
   of a 422: the date arithmetic overflows. Confirmed by running it.
  Severity (reviewer): low
  My take: Agree, trivial fix: reject an as-of outside a sane range (e.g. before

  2000 or after 2100) with a 422, plus one API test.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Finding: The threshold guard accepts MIN_BASELINE_MEDIAN ≤ 0 (divide-by-zero, 
  a
   500) and a negative RANGE_TRIM (an inverted range that flags normal weeks).
  Confirmed by running it.
  Severity (reviewer): low
  My take: Agree, and it matters more than "low" because D10 says product will
  tune these constants. Validate min_baseline_median > 0, range_trim ≥ 0 and
  min_flag_distance > 0, with rule tests. Cheap.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Finding: The name tie-break uses plain string order, so "Site 10" sorts before

  "Site 9".
  Severity (reviewer): low
  My take: Real but cosmetic: it only affects ties and same-group alphabetical
  order, and the seed only uses letters. Natural sort is a one-liner. Optional.

  The reviewer also noted performance points (the account's whole history is
  converted on every request, and the accounts query runs DISTINCT over the full
  table) and style points (SQL in seed.py, event types listed in five places).
  It left them off the list because none produces a wrong result. The seed.py
  one does break your "SQL lives in relay/weekly.py" convention, though arguably
  the seed loader is exempt.

  If you want my ranking: 5, 4 and 1(a) are worth fixing before submitting; each
  is small and test-first. 2 is a README wording fix. 3 is a copy tweak at
  most. 6 is optional.

  Tell me which ones to fix.

  ⎿  Updated README.md (+3 -1)
      76  - **Raw SQL for the aggregation, Alembic's schema operations fo
          r the schema.** The weekly aggregation (removing duplicates, lo
          cal-time conversion, `generate_series` to fill empty weeks) is 
          one readable query with bound parameters in `relay/weekly.py`. 
          An ORM would hide the part that most needs to be visible. The s
          chema, indexes and the duplicate-free view `activity_events_ded
          up` are Alembic migrations.
      77  - **SQL buckets the data; pure Python applies the rule.** `rela
          y/normality.py` has no database access and uses exact `Fraction
          ` arithmetic, so a distance of exactly 0.3 is flagged.
      78  - **The URL is the only UI state.** Reload, share and back/forw
          ard all work, and there's no second copy of the state to drift.
           **The frontend renders and never ranks:** the API returns loca
          tions already ordered, with an explicit `flagged` field.
      79 +- **Sparklines are plain SVG, no chart library.** Each is 9 poi
         +nts, one shaded band (the typical range) and one line, plus a h
         +over tooltip. A library would be a large dependency for that, a
         +nd plain SVG reuses the WCAG-checked colour tokens and is testa
         +ble with Testing Library. Each chart is scaled to its own data 
         +and band. Week series come from the API, so the frontend still 
         +computes nothing. (PLAN.md §6 said no charts in v1; these were 
         +added after the UI review.)                                    
      80  
      81  ## How the numbers were verified
      82  
     ...
       96  1. Tune `MIN_FLAG_DISTANCE` and `MIN_BASELINE_MEDIAN` with pro
           duct, using the examples below and in "Try these".
       97  2. Add the missed-call rate per account, with a minimum number

       98  3. Make the default as-of the last week that has ended in ever

           e limitation below).
       98 -4. Add a 9-week sparkline per location.

          +stop per chart, arrow keys between weeks).                    
      100  5. Set up CI that runs `docker compose up` from scratch, both 
           test suites, and one browser smoke test.
      101  
      102  ## Known limitations and open questions
      103  
      104  - **Account totals** (not defined in PLAN.md) are the account'
           s own weekly counts per event type. Every event counts, includ
           ing a new location's partial first week, so this week's total 
           still equals the sum of the rows. They use the same median and
            range rule. They're context only and never flagged, even when
            well outside their range: Pacific Smiles appointments were 9 
           vs typical 4–6 in the week of Jul 13.
      105  - **The default as-of is the UTC date of the latest event (D2)
           .** If data arrives up to a Sunday evening in a US timezone, t
           he UTC date is already Monday, so the default picks a week tha
           t hasn't ended locally and can falsely flag it below typical. 
           The seed isn't affected. Switching to today's date doesn't fix
            this if "today" is the UTC date: it has the same flaw. The de
           fault must pick the last week that has ended in every account'
           s timezone (for example, using today's date in the westernmost
            zone).
      106 +- **Sparkline tooltips are mouse-only.** Every week's count is
          + in each chart's accessible label, so screen readers get all o
          +f it, but a sighted keyboard user can't see past weeks' counts
          +. This week's count and the range are always visible as text. 
      107  - **Questions for product:** see [PLAN.md §3](PLAN.md). Two mo
           re came up in review:
      108    - **The 0.3 threshold sits close to cases a person would cal
           l worrying.** Account 6 Site N, week of Apr 13: 1 event vs typ
           ical 3–8, distance 2/7 ≈ 0.29, so only "slightly below".
      109    - **Above and below typical share one highlight on purpose.*
           * Whether more activity is good depends on the business.

● The sparklines are in, in each location row and for the account total in the
  header. All 134 backend and 24 frontend tests pass. Everything is committed
  locally (6 commits) but not pushed; the public repo is still at 34264cb.

  Plain SVG or a library: plain SVG, no new dependency.
  - Each chart is 9 points, one shaded band and one line, plus a tooltip, which
    fits in one ~90-line component.
  - A chart library would be a large dependency for that, and its axes and
    tooltip would mostly need switching off. My earlier "~100 KB gzipped" for
    Recharts was from memory, not measured.
  - Plain SVG reuses the colour tokens we already checked for contrast, and
    Testing Library can query it like any other part of the page.
  - This follows your dependency rule: nothing new in package.json.

  What it looks like:
  - Past weeks are a 2px line in muted ink, and this week is an 8px blue dot.
  - The typical range is a light shaded band behind the line.
  - Hovering a week shows a tooltip like "Mar 30–Apr 5: 8 events". Each week's
    hover area is the full chart height, so it's easy to hit.
  - Weeks before a location's history are left blank, and with no baseline
    there's no band.
  - The header has a new line, "All events 55 (typical 71–84)", with its own
    larger sparkline.
  - I checked the colours with the dataviz skill's palette validator in light
    and dark mode:
    - Dark mode: my first accent was too close to the grey line, so I changed it
      to #3b82f6.
    - The grey line: it fails the validator's "reads as grey" check on purpose;
      the guidance wants past weeks de-emphasised.

  Where the data comes from: the API sends each location's 9 weeks (start, end,
  count, null before history) and an account_total, so the frontend still
  computes nothing. The account total uses the same median and range rule and is
  context only, never flagged.

  How it was built (same order as before):
  - The reference script was extended first, and its output gave the seed
    answers: Site G's 9 weeks, Site C's, and the account total for 4 cases.
  - The failing backend tests came next (12 failing), then the code, with two
    injected bugs both caught.
  - Then 4 failing frontend tests, then the component.

  Fixed after looking at the rendered page:
  - The sparklines weren't lined up across rows, because each row sized its
    count column to its own text. The columns now have fixed widths.
  - The header sparkline was squashed flat with a scale starting at 0, since the
    totals run 55–90. Each chart now scales to its own data and band.
  - In dark mode the band was nearly invisible on the flagged rows, so its
    opacity is higher there.

  Limitation, recorded in the README: the tooltip only works with a mouse. Every
  week's count is in the chart's accessible label, so screen readers get all of
  it, but a sighted keyboard user can't see past weeks' counts. One tab stop
  per chart with arrow keys between weeks is listed under "With another day". I
  can build it now if you'd rather.

  README:
  - It explains the plain-SVG choice and notes that PLAN.md §6 said "no charts
    in v1".
  - "With another day" swaps the sparkline item for keyboard access.
  - The screenshots are regenerated.
  - The README grew by about 8% (1,770 to 1,906 words).

  Verify: Site G's series (6, 7, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8, 2, 0), account 6's total of 55
  with typical 71–84 (median 157/2), and account 15's total of 20 with typical
  15–24 all come from analysis/reference.py output. The seed tests assert them.
  The tooltip value "Mar 30–Apr 5: 8 events" was read from the live page, and it
  matches the reference's 7th week for Site G.

  ⎿  79:- **Sparklines are plain SVG, no chart library.** Each is 9 points,

  ⎿  Updated README.md (+2 -1)
      76  - **Raw SQL for the aggregation, Alembic's schema operations fo
          r the schema.** The weekly aggregation (removing duplicates, lo
          cal-time conversion, `generate_series` to fill empty weeks) is 
          one readable query with bound parameters in `relay/weekly.py`. 
          An ORM would hide the part that most needs to be visible. The s
          chema, indexes and the duplicate-free view `activity_events_ded
          up` are Alembic migrations.
      77  - **SQL buckets the data; pure Python applies the rule.** `rela
          y/normality.py` has no database access and uses exact `Fraction
          ` arithmetic, so a distance of exactly 0.3 is flagged.
      78  - **The URL is the only UI state.** Reload, share and back/forw
          ard all work, and there's no second copy of the state to drift.
           **The frontend renders and never ranks:** the API returns loca
          tions already ordered, with an explicit `flagged` field.
      79 -- **Sparklines are plain SVG, no chart library.** Each is 9 poi
         -nts, one shaded band (the typical range) and one line, plus a h

  How it was built:

    weeks where the types don't add up to the total.

    up" test over every account at three as-of dates, a hand-worked synthetic
    case with mixed types, and the Site G and account 6 per-type series from the
    reference.

      instead of tuples). Their expected values didn't change.
  - Then the code: the API's weekly points gain by_event_type, null before
    history.

    zeros instead of null before history failed 3. Using lowercase labels in the
    tooltip failed 2 frontend tests.

  One thing worth knowing about "always adds up": it holds because the data only
  has the three event types. If an unknown event_type ever appeared, it would
  count in the total without a per-type bucket. A CHECK constraint migration, or
  an "other" bucket, would make the guarantee structural. Tell me if you want
  either.
